					<description><![CDATA[<p>2D Tunnel FET; in-memory photonic computing; India's $13.4B Semicon 2.0; SEMICON Taiwan; PCIe 6/7 test; MediaTek deal; 300mm Si photonics; InP expansion; $75M to recycle rare earths; GF PDKs; Gain-Cell RAM; edge AI developer tools;  heterogeneous HBM; safe AI in SDVs.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://semiengineering.com/chip-industry-week-in-review-154/">Chip Industry Week In Review</a> appeared first on <a href="https://semiengineering.com">Semiconductor Engineering</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[<h2><strong>Innovations</strong></h2>
<p><strong>Hong Kong Polytechnic University</strong>-led researchers developed a <a href="https://www.science.org/doi/10.1126/science.adx6059">2D tunnel transistor</a> that can switch at lower voltages than conventional MOSFETs while maintaining strong current performance. The device was demonstrated at room temperature on centimeter-scale silicon substrates and meets IRDS requirements for next-gen integrated circuits, pointing toward lower-power future chips.</p>
<p><strong>Tsinghua University</strong> researchers developed a <a href="https://www.nature.com/articles/s44460-026-00123-2">fully in-memory photonic computing architecture</a> that combines sensing, processing, and memory on a 7,378-neuron multicore chip. The system was demonstrated on autonomous racing-drone navigation and is designed to reduce data movement between computation and external memory.</p>
<h2><strong>Analysis</strong></h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>SEMI</strong> reported that g<a href="https://www.semi.org/en/semi-press-release/global-semiconductor-equipment-billings-increased-23-percent-year-over-year-in-q2-2026-semi-reports">lobal semiconductor equipment billing</a>s increased 23% YoY to $40.53B in Q2 2026.</li>
<li><strong>SIA</strong> released its <a href="https://www.semiconductors.org/2026-state-of-the-u-s-semiconductor-industry/">State of the U.S. Semiconductor Industry 2026 report</a>, highlighting increased sales, demand drivers, new investments, and supply chain challenges.</li>
<li><strong>McKinsey &amp; Company</strong> suggests that AI could improve build processes and operations within <a href="https://www.mckinsey.com/industries/semiconductors/our-insights/revolutionizing-semiconductor-fab-operations-with-ai">semiconductor fabs</a>, potentially saving nearly $134B in functional costs over 5 to 7 years.</li>
</ul>
<p><img data-recalc-dims="1" fetchpriority="high" decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-24281021" src="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/SEMI-equipment.jpeg?resize=1360%2C876&#038;ssl=1" alt="" width="1360" height="876" srcset="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/SEMI-equipment.jpeg?w=1360&amp;ssl=1 1360w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/SEMI-equipment.jpeg?resize=300%2C193&amp;ssl=1 300w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/SEMI-equipment.jpeg?resize=1024%2C660&amp;ssl=1 1024w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/SEMI-equipment.jpeg?resize=768%2C495&amp;ssl=1 768w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/SEMI-equipment.jpeg?resize=600%2C386&amp;ssl=1 600w" sizes="(max-width: 1000px) 100vw, 1000px" /></p>
<p><strong>Fig. 1: Semiconductor equipment billings by region. Source: SEMI and SEAJ, September 2026</strong></p>
<h3><strong>Photonics</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>MIT </strong>and <strong>NY CREATES</strong> researchers developed a <a href="https://news.mit.edu/2026/fabrication-platform-could-enable-flexible-transparent-next-generation-photonic-chips-0903">300-mm manufacturing process for flexible, transparent silicon photonics</a> using sndard semiconductor fabrication techniques. The devices maintained their performance after thousands of bending cycles, potentially enabling new applications such as wearable sensors and curved augmented-reality displays.</li>
<li><span data-contrast="none"><a href="https://semiengineering.com/photonics-forces-a-chiplet-rethink/">Optical chiplets</a> promise a way out of the bandwidth, power, and reach limits of copper interconnects, but bringing photonics into advanced packages is proving more difficult than just swapping one signaling medium for another. </span><span data-ccp-props="{&quot;201341983&quot;:0,&quot;335559739&quot;:0,&quot;335559740&quot;:240}"> </span><span data-ccp-props="{&quot;201341983&quot;:0,&quot;335559739&quot;:0,&quot;335559740&quot;:240}"> </span></li>
<li>Counterpoint reports on a <a href="https://counterpointresearch.com/en/insights/hot-interconnect-new-form-factors-fibers-use-cases-optics">faster shift toward optical</a> connectivity, with NPO, next-gen pluggables, and multicore fiber likely to see broader adoption before co-packaged optics.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>SEMICON Taiwan</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Teradyne</strong> launched three new <a href="https://investors.teradyne.com/news-events/press-releases/detail/447/teradyne-launches-advanced-ultraflexplus-instruments-engineered-for-ai-and-data-center-computing-devices">test instruments</a>: a digital instrument built for pattern-intensive AI and compute devices; a high-speed I/O protocol ATE solution for PCIe Gen6 interfaces; and a power supply that can deliver 1280 amps per instrument.</li>
<li>Taiwanese companies could spend <a href="https://www.taipeitimes.com/News/biz/archives/2026/09/04/2003863636">$20B on U.S. investment</a>, the country&#8217;s economy minister said at the opening of the U.S. pavilion at the Semicon trade show in Taipei, per Taipei Times.</li>
<li><strong>SEMI</strong> and <strong>Silicon Catalyst</strong> <a href="https://siliconcatalyst.com/semi-and-silicon-catalyst-aim-to-accelerate-global-semiconductor-innovation">inked an MoU</a> to connect each organization&#8217;s partner companies with the aim of accelerating the commercialization of breakthrough technologies.</li>
<li><strong>Qnity Electronics</strong> introduced a family of <a href="https://www.qnityelectronics.com/news/qnity-introduces-stackplane-cmp-slurry-platform.html">slurries</a> for chemical mechanical planarization (CMP) steps in advanced packaging processes.</li>
<li><strong>Trumpf</strong> uncorked an <a href="https://www.trumpf.com/en_US/newsroom/global-press-releases/press-release-detail-page/release/semicon-taiwan-trumpf-enables-integrated-chip-cooling-for-the-next-generation-of-ai-chips-10289/">ultrashort-pulse laser</a> application that ablates silicon carbide to enable cooling systems integrated into AI chips.</li>
<li><strong>Mixx Technologies</strong> released an <a href="https://mixxtech.io/mixx-launches-sxctm-connector-for-high-radix-scale-up-and-multi-petabit-connectivity/">optical connector</a> for both the front plane and the backplane that terminates up to 24,576 fibers in a single rack unit.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>Big deals</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Nvidia</strong> will <a href="https://blogs.nvidia.com/blog/nvidia-to-acquire-hugging-face/">acquire</a> <strong>Hugging Face</strong>, a platform for open-weight AI model deployment, for $12.9B. Nvidia already offers its models on the platform, which will remain open for the entire AI ecosystem without restrictions on underlying compute.</li>
<li>Nvidia also bought $3.5B worth of <strong>MediaTek</strong> <a href="https://www.mediatek.com/press-room/nvidia-and-mediatek-deepen-long-standing-partnership-to-build-ai-edge-to-cloud-computing-platforms">convertible bonds</a>. MediaTek will offer the NVLink Fusion platform as a design foundation for custom AI accelerator customers, and the two companies will continue collaborating in edge and automotive.</li>
<li><strong>Lyte</strong> raised $165M to scale production of custom silicon, multimodal sensors, and <a href="https://lyte.ai/news/series-c">spatial software for robots</a>.</li>
<li><strong>iPronics</strong> raised $125M to scale up silicon photonics-based <a href="https://ipronics.com/ipronics-raises-125-million-to-scale-programmable-optical-networking-for-ai-data-centers/">optical circuit switching</a> that enables AI clusters to reconfigure connectivity in real time for training and inference workloads.</li>
</ul>
<h3 role="heading" aria-level="2" data-uw-rm-heading="level"><strong>Earnings:</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li>YoY revenue growth: <a href="https://investors.broadcom.com/news-releases/news-release-details/broadcom-inc-announces-third-quarter-fiscal-year-2026-financial">Broadcom</a> (up 86%), <a href="https://investors.credosemi.com/news-events/news/news-details/2026/Credo-Technology-Group-Holding-Ltd-Reports-First-Quarter-of-Fiscal-Year-2027-Financial-Results/default.aspx">Credo</a> (up 115%), and <a href="https://www.reuters.com/world/asia-pacific/chinas-cxmt-supply-memory-chip-xiaomis-upcoming-folding-phone-2026-08-29/">CXMT</a> (up 874% first half).</li>
</ul>

<h2 id="Global"><strong>Global</strong></h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>India</strong> <a href="https://d2p5j06zete1i7.cloudfront.net/Cms/admin/PressRelease/1788180277.pdf">launched</a> its ~$13.4B Semicon 2.0 program with <a href="https://ism.gov.in/schemes/semicon2.0/machines-and-materials">six pillars</a> from design to fabs and talent. Bloomberg reported the country will subsidize up to <a href="https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-03-12/india-plans-new-11-billion-fund-to-support-local-chipmaking">50% of project costs</a> for small and startup chip design firms and support capital spending on plants to produce and refurbish chipmaking equipment, as well as advanced and legacy packaging efforts.</li>
<li><strong>Sivers Semiconductors</strong> will spend $30M to expand its <a href="https://www.sivers-semiconductors.com/press/sivers-semiconductors-invests-usd-30-million-to-expand-european-photonics-manufacturing-for-ai-datacenters/">indium phosphide</a> manufacturing facility in Glasgow, <strong>Scotland</strong>, to support annual production of more than 100 million CW DFB lasers for optical networking.</li>
<li><strong>Boise State University</strong> and the <strong>University of Idaho</strong> received an $8M <strong>NSF</strong> grant to research microelectronics for <a href="https://www.boisestate.edu/news/2026/08/31/8-million-grant-sparks-xtreme-project-to-position-idaho-as-leader-in-resilient-microelectronics/">extreme environments</a>.</li>
<li>The <strong>University of Arizona</strong> and <strong>National Sun Yat-sen University</strong> launched a <a href="https://news.arizona.edu/news/new-global-hub-advances-arizonas-semiconductor-industry">research and education</a> center focused on silicon photonics.</li>
</ul>

<h2 id="Reports and Deals"><strong>Reports and Deals</strong></h2>
<h3><strong>Deals</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>proteanTecs</strong>&#8216; embedded <a href="https://www.proteantecs.com/pressroom/proteantecs-and-megachips-collaborate-to-bring-health-and-performance-management-solutions-to-next-generation-lsi-designs">health and performance monitoring</a> technology will be integrated into <strong>MegaChips</strong>&#8216; next-generation ASIC and LSI digital systems.</li>
<li><b>NanoBridge Semiconductor</b> adopted <b>Siemens</b>’ <span draggable="true"><a href="https://news.siemens.com/en-us/siemens-precision-fpga-nanobridge/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">FPGA logic synthesis</a></span> technology for its low-power and harsh-environment FPGA device families.</li>
<li><strong>Baya Systems</strong> teamed up with <strong>AdoreSys</strong> to offer its early architectural exploration and optimization platform and <a href="https://bayasystems.com/press-releases/baya-systems-chooses-adoresys-as-partner-to-expand-semiconductor-ip-availability-across-china-and-asia-pacific/">fabric IP</a> alongside ASIC design and implementation services.</li>
<li><strong>Infineon </strong>and <strong>Skeleton Technologies </strong>will collaborate on high-efficiency, resilient solid-state transformers and sidecar <a href="https://www.infineon.com/technology-news/2026/inftn202609-134">power solutions for modern data centers</a>.</li>
<li><strong>mPTech </strong>integrated Infineon’s SECORA <a href="https://www.infineon.com/technology-news/2026/inftn202609-135">secure payment technology</a> into its latest flagship Hammer smartwatch.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>M&amp;A, launches</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li>AI software company <strong>MulticoreWare</strong> <a href="https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/multicoreware-acquires-simulus-automation-to-expand-semiconductor-engineering-capabilities-302867811.html">acquired</a> <strong>Simulus Automation</strong>, a provider of design verification, protocol IP, FPGA development, and engineering services.</li>
<li><strong>CollPlant Biotechnologies</strong> <a href="https://ir.collplant.com/news-events/press-releases/detail/202/collplant-is-acquiring-lightsolver--enters-supercomputing-and-photonic-computing-markets">acquired</a> photonic computing startup <strong>LightSolver</strong> to expand beyond biotech.</li>
<li><strong>Pasqal</strong> listed on Nasdaq following the completion of its <a href="https://www.pasqal.com/newsroom/pasqal-and-bleichroeder-acquisition-corp-ii-complete-business-combination/">merger</a> with SPAC <strong>Bleichroeder</strong>.</li>
<li><strong>Diodes Incorporated</strong> completed its $250M <a href="https://www.diodes.com/about/news/press-releases/diodes-completes-acquisition-of-elevate-semiconductor-expanding-presence-in-high-growth-ate-market">acquisition</a> of <strong>ElevATE Semiconductor</strong>, a designer of AMS chips for the ATE market.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>More funding</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Cyclic Materials</strong> raised $75M to recycle <a href="https://cyclicmaterials.earth/resources/cyclic-materials-announces-usd-75-million-strategic-growth-financing-to-scale-u-s-rare-earth-production">rare earths</a> and critical minerals used in semiconductor, automotive, and other sectors.</li>
<li><strong>Diffraqtion</strong> added $5.8M to build <a href="https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/lockheed-martin-ventures-and-sumitomos-presidio-ventures-back-diffraqtion-302864959.html">quantum cameras</a> that resolve detail below the diffraction limit for defense and space sensing.</li>
<li><strong>Zenergize Technologies</strong> raised $4M for <a href="https://zenergize.in/news/zenergize-raises-usd-4-million-in-pre-series-a-to-accelerate-to-100cr">SiC power electronics</a> for EV chargers and solar inverters.</li>
<li><strong>S-Transistors</strong> raised €2.6M (~$3M) to build cryogenic signal control and handling ICs based on <a href="https://www.vttresearch.com/en/news-and-ideas/s-transistors-raises-eu26-million-pre-seed-round-introduce-unique-superconducting">superconducting transistors</a>.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>Reports, Opinions</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://www.yolegroup.com/press-release/photonics-at-the-speed-of-ai-yole-group-charts-the-industrys-shift-from-compute-to-communication/">Optical transceivers</a> have become a system-level requirement for scaling AI, with the market expected to grow to $112B in 2031 (<strong>Yole</strong>)</li>
<li>Surge in <a href="https://www.anaqua.com/resource/new-anaqua-report-reveals-surge-in-ai-semiconductor-patent-filings/">AI Semiconductor Patent Filings</a> (<strong>Anaqua</strong>)</li>
<li>The search for <a href="https://www.economist.com/interactive/briefing/2026/08/20/the-search-for-consciousness-inside-llms">consciousness inside AI</a> (<strong>Economist</strong>)</li>
<li>N. American <a href="https://www.electronics.org/news-release/north-american-pcb-demand-strengthens-july-bookings-surge-62">PCB Demand Strengthens</a> (<strong>Global Electronics</strong>)</li>
<li><a href="https://counterpointresearch.com/en/insights/the-trillion-dollar-bottleneck-nvidias-nvhbm-fight-for-base-die-blog">Nvidia&#8217;s NVHBM</a> and the Fight for the Base Die (<strong>Counterpoint</strong>)</li>
<li>The Race to Win the <a href="https://www.asiahouse.org/2026/09/02/the-race-to-win-the-great-semiconductor-realignment-new-asia-house-report/">Great Semiconductor Realignment</a> (<strong>Asia House</strong>)</li>
</ul>

<h2><a href="https://vitrek.com/precision-control-in-semiconductor-wafer-lapping/?utm_source=%09+M-Semiconductor+Engineering-Week+in+Review+Enewsletter-Wafer+Lapping-2026-06&amp;utm_medium=%09+M-Semiconductor+Engineering-Week+in+Review+Enewsletter-Wafer+Lapping-2026-06&amp;utm_campaign=%09+M-Semiconductor+Engineering-Week+in+Review+Enewsletter-Wafer+Lapping-2026-06&amp;utm_term=%09+M-Semiconductor+Engineering-Week+in+Review+Enewsletter-Wafer+Lapping-2026-06&amp;utm_content=%09+M-Semiconductor+Engineering-Week+in+Review+Enewsletter-Wafer+Lapping-2026-06"><img data-recalc-dims="1" decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-24275815 size-full" src="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Vitrek-May26-News-alert-wafer-lapping-WP-article-banner-1270x240-1.jpg?resize=1270%2C240&#038;ssl=1" alt="" width="1270" height="240" srcset="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Vitrek-May26-News-alert-wafer-lapping-WP-article-banner-1270x240-1.jpg?w=1270&amp;ssl=1 1270w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Vitrek-May26-News-alert-wafer-lapping-WP-article-banner-1270x240-1.jpg?resize=300%2C57&amp;ssl=1 300w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Vitrek-May26-News-alert-wafer-lapping-WP-article-banner-1270x240-1.jpg?resize=1024%2C194&amp;ssl=1 1024w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Vitrek-May26-News-alert-wafer-lapping-WP-article-banner-1270x240-1.jpg?resize=768%2C145&amp;ssl=1 768w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Vitrek-May26-News-alert-wafer-lapping-WP-article-banner-1270x240-1.jpg?resize=600%2C113&amp;ssl=1 600w" sizes="(max-width: 1000px) 100vw, 1000px" /></a></h2>
<hr />
<h2 id="New Technologies"><strong>New Technologies</strong></h2>
<h3><strong>Manufacturing, test</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Bruker</strong> unveiled a large-area <a href="https://www.bruker.com/en/news-and-events/news/2026/bruker-drives-major-advances-in-detector-technology-for-semiconductor-and-nanostructure-analysis.html">Energy-Dispersive Spectroscopy detector</a> for scanning electron microscopy with the count-rate performance of four independently operating Silicon Drift Detector segments.</li>
<li><strong>Rigaku</strong> debuted a high-resolution <a href="https://rigaku-holdings.com/english/news/corporate/2026-09-01/press-release-rigaku-launches-ct-lab-hr160-high-resolution-x-ray-ct-system-developed-in-collaboration-with-excillum-for-battery-semiconductor-devices-and-advanced-materials-analysis/">X-ray CT</a> system for battery, semiconductor devices, electronic components, and advanced materials analysis.</li>
<li><strong>GlobalFoundries</strong> announced <a href="https://gf.com/news-and-events/news/gf-announces-customer-availability-of-ux-platform-technologies-for-intelligent-edge-connectivity-and-physical-ai-applications/">PDK availability</a> for its 22nm and 40nm UX CMOS processes built for intelligent edge devices and advanced sensing systems.</li>
<li><strong>GF</strong> and <strong>RAAAM Memory Technologies</strong> <a href="https://gf.com/news-and-events/news/gf-and-raaam-memory-technologies-announce-the-joint-development-of-next-generation-gcram-technology-and-test-chip-on-fdx-platform/">taped out</a> a Gain-Cell RAM (GCRAM) test vehicle on GF&#8217;s FDX fully depleted silicon-on-insulator (FD-SOI) platform.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>Interconnects/interfaces</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Cadence</strong>&#8216;s <a href="https://newsroom.cadence.com/press-releases/press-release-details/2026/Cadence-Subsystem-for-PCIe-6-0-Architecture-Achieves-First-Pass-PCI-Express-Specification-Compliance/default.aspx">PCIe 6.0 PHY and controller IP</a>, implemented in the TSMC N3 process, achieved 64 GT/s specification compliance.</li>
<li><strong>EverProX</strong> uncorked an ultra-low-power MIPI <a href="https://www.silicon-line.com/silicon-line-an-everprox-brand-launches-new-ultra-low-power-serdes-chipset-msdl-mipi-serial-data-link-extending-20-gbps-mipi-d-phy-connectivity-to-tens-of-meters/">SerDes chipset for vision</a> systems.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>Design</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Andes Technology</strong> deployed <strong>ChipAgents</strong>&#8216; <a href="https://www.andestech.com/tw/2026/09/02/chipagents-helps-andes-technology-dramatically-accelerate-custom-processor-design-and-verification-2/">agentic AI platform</a> across multiple processor design and verification projects, reducing a processor customization task by 30% and a separate verification workflow from 2 to 3 months to about 3 weeks.</li>
<li><strong>MIPS</strong> uncorked several <a href="https://mips.com/press-releases/mips-launches-workload-native-platforms-for-physical-ai/">developer platforms</a> for edge inference, event-driven embedded, and physical AI.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong><a href="https://www.siemens.com/en-us/campaigns/calibre-ic-design/?utm_campaign=2026-8-global-calibre_leadership_awareness&amp;utm_source=semiconductor_engineering&amp;utm_medium=content_network&amp;cmpid=119513"><img data-recalc-dims="1" decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-24279950 size-full" src="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Static-08-26-sie-banner-calibre-1270x240px-performance_V1_static.jpg.jpeg?resize=1270%2C240&#038;ssl=1" alt="" width="1270" height="240" srcset="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Static-08-26-sie-banner-calibre-1270x240px-performance_V1_static.jpg.jpeg?w=1270&amp;ssl=1 1270w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Static-08-26-sie-banner-calibre-1270x240px-performance_V1_static.jpg.jpeg?resize=300%2C57&amp;ssl=1 300w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Static-08-26-sie-banner-calibre-1270x240px-performance_V1_static.jpg.jpeg?resize=1024%2C194&amp;ssl=1 1024w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Static-08-26-sie-banner-calibre-1270x240px-performance_V1_static.jpg.jpeg?resize=768%2C145&amp;ssl=1 768w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Static-08-26-sie-banner-calibre-1270x240px-performance_V1_static.jpg.jpeg?resize=600%2C113&amp;ssl=1 600w" sizes="(max-width: 1000px) 100vw, 1000px" /></a></strong></h3>
<hr />
<h2 id="Research"><strong>Research</strong></h2>
<p><strong>EPFL</strong> researchers demonstrated a <a href="https://www.nature.com/articles/s41928-026-01691-4">GaN-on-Si transistor using a polarization superjunction</a> that sustained more than 3.4 kV, along with diodes that exceeded 3.9 kV.</p>
<p><strong>Seoul National University</strong>-led researchers developed an AI method that separates overlapping <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30410">semiconductor defect patterns</a> at the pixel level using only image-level defect labels.</p>
<h3><strong>More research</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://semiengineering.com/workload-driven-hbf-substrate-for-capacity-scalable-llm-inference-huawei-eth-zurich-hust/">Leveraging HBF</a> for Capacity-Scalable LLM Inference Acceleration (<strong>Huawei</strong> et al.)</li>
<li>HW-Managed <a href="https://semiengineering.com/hybrid-hbm-hbf-architecture-in-llm-inference-university-of-oxford/">Heterogeneous HBM and Flash</a> in LLM Inference Systems (<strong>Oxford</strong>)</li>
</ul>
<hr />
<h2 id="Security"><strong>Security</strong></h2>
<h3><strong>Tools and guidance</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>NIST </strong>published a set of <a href="https://csrc.nist.gov/News/2026/next-generation-secure-hardware-into-standards">hardware security recommendations</a> for the national semiconductor industry originating from a Sustainable Hardware Security Workshop held in January. The report outlines “unified, interoperable, and evidence-based trust models, vocabularies, and guidance” for the industry.</li>
<li><strong>Nvidia </strong>and <strong>CrowdStrike </strong>announced SafeMind, an <a href="https://blogs.nvidia.com/blog/nvidia-crowdstrike-fal-con-2026/">agentic cybersecurity system</a> developed using Nvidia’s Nemotron open models by the CrowdStrike Cyber Superintelligence Lab<strong>.</strong></li>
<li>CrowdStrike also introduced Falcon Guardian, its new <a href="https://ir.crowdstrike.com/news-releases/news-release-details/crowdstrike-unveils-falcon-guardian-secure-ai-agents-where-they">AI detection and response</a> cybersecurity infrastructure solution.</li>
<li><strong>Google</strong> launched the <a href="https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/technology/safety-security/fairwind-program/">Fairwind Program</a>, a cybersecurity collaboration giving its customers, government agencies, and cybersecurity partners access to Google’s advanced AI and cyber defense capabilities.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>Threats</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li>A federal grand jury in <strong>California</strong> indicted a <strong>Russian</strong> national accused of <a href="https://www.justice.gov/usao-ndca/pr/russian-national-indicted-exploiting-online-platform-used-freelance-employment-and">distributing</a> <a href="https://www.justice.gov/usao-ndca/pr/russian-national-indicted-exploiting-online-platform-used-freelance-employment-and">malware to roughly 80,000 users</a> of a freelance employment platform over the course of a years-long cybercrime scheme.</li>
<li>AI-enabled vulnerability discovery and exploitation was the <a href="https://www.gartner.com/en/newsroom/press-releases/2026-08-25-use-this-gartner-survey-finds-ai-discovery-vulnerabilities-top-emerging-risk">top emerging risk</a> in Q2 2026, based on a survey of 316 executives and risk managers, reports <strong>Gartner</strong>.</li>
<li>As AI-driven cryptanalysis, post-quantum migration, chiplets, and custom silicon reshape the threat landscape, the semiconductor industry is confronting <span draggable="true"><a href="https://semiengineering.com/security-sign-off-is-coming-for-chips-but-standards-may-not-be-enough/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">how to define, verify, and sign off security</a></span> across increasingly complex chips and systems.</li>
<li>Securing the entire <a href="https://semiengineering.com/ai-is-forcing-data-centers-to-rethink-trust/">data center supply chain</a> is a monumental task, and the stakes keep rising as the value of data stored in hyperscale facilities increases by the minute. With 800V DC architectures, <a href="https://semiengineering.com/what-can-go-wrong-in-800vdc-ai-data-centers/">safety and reliability challenges</a> also increase.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>Security technical papers</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li>Extracting LLM Assets from <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25321">Edge AI Chips</a> (<strong>WPI</strong>)</li>
<li>A HW-in-the-Loop Optimizer for <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00502">Physical Fault Injection</a> (<strong>Keysight</strong>)</li>
<li>HW Keystores for AI Agent Signing Workflows: A Zero-Trust <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06130">MCP Enforcement Architecture</a> (<strong>Hardware Systems Security Lab, Huawei</strong>, et al.)</li>
<li>Workload Identification with <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00309v1">Physical Side Channels for AI Governance</a> (<strong>Pivotal Research, Intelligence Security Laboratories</strong>)</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>Upcoming security event </strong></h3>
<ul>
<li>The <strong>ESD Alliance</strong> will host a Sept. 10 <a href="https://www.semi.org/en/event/esd-alliance-webinar-gen-ai-chip-design-and-security">webinar on genAI for chip design and hardware security</a>, with <strong>Warren Savage</strong> and University of Florida’s <strong>Mark Tehranipoor</strong> discussing multi-agent AI for SoC design, threat modeling, and security verification.</li>
</ul>
<hr />
<h2 id="Vehicles, Batteries"><strong>Vehicles</strong></h2>
<h3><strong>Partnerships</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Keysight Technologies </strong>is collaborating with the Centre for Assuring Autonomy at the <strong>University of York,</strong> focused on the <a href="https://www.keysight.com/us/en/about/newsroom/catalog/news-release.2026.0903_pr26-092_keysight-collaborates-with-the-centre-for-assuring-autonomy-at-the-university-of-york-to-advance-ai-safety-assurance-for-software-defined-vehicles.html">safe deployment of AI in SDVs</a> (software-defined vehicles), including developing measurable safety-scoring methodologies and creating practical frameworks for generating auditable AI safety evidence.</li>
<li><strong>Nissan </strong>and <strong>Honda </strong>agreed to create <a href="https://global.nissannews.com/en/releases/260831-01-e">standardized ECUs for next-gen SDVs</a> from fiscal year 2029 onward.</li>
<li><strong>Nexperia </strong>will collaborate with <strong>Aurobay Technologies </strong>on <a href="https://www.nexperia.com/about/news-events/press-releases/nexperia-explores-collaboration-on-next-generation-power-semiconductor-solutions-for-electrified-powertrains-with-aurobay-technologies">advanced power vehicle semiconductor</a> solutions, combining Nexperia’s expertise in GaN and SiC with Aurobay’s capabilities in powertrain system design and integration.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>AVs, EVs </strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Zoox </strong>and <strong>Waymo </strong>each expanded robotaxi service to several new U.S. cities this week, with Zoox beginning testing in <a href="https://zoox.com/journal/zoox-now-testing-houston-san-diego">Houston and San Diego</a> and Waymo offering its first public rides in <a href="https://support.google.com/waymo/answer/18106069?sjid=1461513296488936323-NC">Denver, San Diego and Tampa</a>.</li>
<li><strong>LG Innotek </strong>will supply <a href="https://news.lginnotek.com/1665">automotive camera modules</a> for Zoox’s robotaxi fleet in serial production at Zoox’s facility in Hayward, Calif.</li>
<li><strong>Renesas</strong> will bring the Autoware Foundation&#8217;s <a href="https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/autoware-foundation-partners-with-renesas-to-accelerate-production-ready-end-to-end-ai-for-adas-and-autonomous-driving-302866054.html">open-source end-to-end AI software</a> to its R-Car SoC platforms to aid the development of scalable solutions supporting ADAS and full self-driving platforms.</li>
<li><strong>Electrogenic </strong>converted London&#8217;s <a href="https://robbreport.com/motors/cars/london-mayor-turns-1973-rolls-royce-electric-vehicle-1238619371/">1973 Rolls-</a><a href="https://robbreport.com/motors/cars/london-mayor-turns-1973-rolls-royce-electric-vehicle-1238619371/">Royce Phantom limousine</a> into a fully electric vehicle with a new 120 kW electric motor and a 62 kWh battery pack, giving it a range of up to 120 miles.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>eVTOLs, humanoids</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Archer </strong>announced a series of <a href="https://investors.archer.com/news/news-details/2026/Archer-Launches-No-Roads-Flight-Tour-As-Part-Of-Its-Participation-In-White-Houses-Air-Taxi-Pilot-Program-and-Preparation-for-LA28-Games/default.aspx">city-to-city air taxi test flights</a> in California, Florida and Texas as part of its participation in the White House’s eVTOL Integration Pilot Program.</li>
<li>Chip architectures in some <a href="https://semiengineering.com/humanoid-compute-security-more-complex-than-avs/">humanoid robots resemble software-defined, autonomous vehicles</a> with distributed, zonal compute. Others resemble a drone with centralized compute. The common denominator for all kinds of robots is a heterogeneous architecture.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>Sensors</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>FORE Technology</strong> is set to begin mass-producing its line of <span draggable="true"><a href="https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/global-tier-1-yfore-introduces-mass-produced-anti-pinch-sensors-302864500.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">anti-pinch</a></span> automotive sensors at its Suwanee, Ga. facility. The sensors are designed to prevent injuries caused by automatic side doors or lift gates “pinching” a user while closing.</li>
</ul>
<h3><strong>Vehicle technical papers</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19752">Deployment-Aware Testing</a> Pipeline for IoT-Based Automotive (<strong>Mercedes et al</strong>.)</li>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25216">Automotive HSMs</a> &#8211; Architectural Challenges and Security Implications (<strong>University of Detroit Mercy</strong>)</li>
<li>Vision-Based Leader-Follower Formation Control for <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01420">Cooperative UAVs</a> in GPS-Degraded Environments (<strong>AIR Lab</strong>, et al.)</li>
<li>Counterfactual Long-Tail Benchmarking and Knowledge-Preserving Adaptation for <a href="https://academ.us/article/2609.00242/">Driving Affordance Prediction</a> (<strong>Nvidia</strong>)</li>
</ul>
<hr />
<h2 id="Quantum"><strong>Quantum</strong></h2>
<p><strong>IBM</strong> announced its latest <a href="https://www.ibm.com/quantum/blog/nighthawk-r2">quantum processor</a>, which has 120 programmable qubits and a high-speed qubit reset architecture that is capable of executing over 100,000 circuits per second.</p>
<p><strong>Xanadu</strong> updated its <a href="https://investors.xanadu.ai/news-releases/news-release-details/xanadu-charts-path-over-1000-logical-qubits-2031">roadmap</a>, aiming to have a fault-tolerant photonic quantum system with up to 200 logical qubits by 2029, and 500 the following year. It will also establish a photonics R&amp;D and manufacturing <a href="https://investors.xanadu.ai/news-releases/news-release-details/xanadu-secures-cad-195-million-and-establishes-landmark-advanced">facility</a> backed by CAD $195M (~$140M) from the Canadian government.</p>
<p><strong>California</strong> plans $9.7M to boost quantum <a href="https://www.gov.ca.gov/2026/08/26/governor-newsom-awards-48-5-million-to-drive-economic-growth-and-advance-innovation-across-the-state-supporting-the-creation-of-20000-new-jobs/">workforce development</a>.</p>
<h3><strong>Quantum technical papers</strong></h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-026-77282-w">Spin-photon interface</a> in the telecom C-band w/long hole spin dephasing time (<strong>Julius-Maximilians-Universität Würzburg</strong>, <strong>KAIST</strong>, et al.)</li>
<li>Automated electrostatic characterization of <a href="https://www.nature.com/articles/s41535-026-00921-4?">quantum dot devices</a> in single- and bilayer heterostructures</li>
</ul>
<hr />
<h2 id="Workforce, Education"><strong>Workforce, Education</strong></h2>
<p><strong>Intel Foundation</strong> and <strong>Project Lead The Way</strong> will grant $3M to expose <a href="https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/newsroom/news/corporate/foundation-pltw-expand-semiconductor-careers.html">high schoolers</a> to advanced manufacturing careers.</p>
<p><strong>Purdue University</strong> is leading a five-university team that received $4.05M over three years in the first SRC SPARC cohort for <a href="https://engineering.purdue.edu/ASIP/news/purdue-leads-a-team-of-five-universities-to-receive-405-million-over-three-years-under-the-first-src-sparc-cohort">advanced packaging and system-integration research</a>.</p>
<p><strong>AMD </strong>and <strong>Delhi University</strong> launched an <a href="https://www.du.ac.in/index.php?cntnt01articleid=18706&amp;cntnt01pagelimit=4&amp;cntnt01returnid=219&amp;mact=News%2Ccntnt01%2Cdetail%2C0">AI training initiative targeting up to 10,000</a> students, with hands-on access to ROCm, technical workshops, and GPU cloud resources.</p>
<p><strong>Pakistan</strong> completed a <a href="https://www.moitt.gov.pk/NewsDetail/MTJmOTg2OGQtOTNlYi00MDZjLTk0MDktNGRiMmQzMTEzYTdm">national semiconductor education</a> network linking regional university clusters under its INSPIRE program, which aims to train 2,200 students and 5,000 engineers in semiconductor skills.</p>

		<item>
		<title>Intelligent Engineering: From Optimization To AI</title>
		<link>https://semiengineering.com/intelligent-engineering-from-optimization-to-ai/</link>
					<comments>https://semiengineering.com/intelligent-engineering-from-optimization-to-ai/#respond</comments>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Bradley Cecil]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 03 Sep 2026 07:08:39 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Blogs - Security]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Building The IoE]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[IoT and Security]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[AI]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[automation]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[data management]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[design space exploration]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[optimization]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[PCIe]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[PPA]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[SerDes]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Siemens EDA]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[signal integrity]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[tradeoffs]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">https://semiengineering.com/?p=24280899</guid>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>A methodology for transforming engineering decisions from educated guesses into data-driven choices.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://semiengineering.com/intelligent-engineering-from-optimization-to-ai/">Intelligent Engineering: From Optimization To AI</a> appeared first on <a href="https://semiengineering.com">Semiconductor Engineering</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>The complexity of modern electronic systems is growing faster than traditional engineering workflows can efficiently manage. High-speed interfaces, dense PCB layouts, advanced packaging technologies, and increasingly stringent performance requirements are forcing organizations to rethink how design decisions are made, validated, and optimized.</p>
<p>This challenge is driving the adoption of intelligent engineering methodologies built upon three interconnected capabilities: optimization, automation, and artificial intelligence (AI). Optimization improves decision quality through systematic exploration of design alternatives. Automation enables those decisions to be executed consistently and efficiently at scale. AI introduces the ability to interpret results, identify patterns, and assist engineers in making informed decisions.</p>
<h2>The growing complexity challenge</h2>
<p>Modern electronic systems are expected to deliver more performance, more connectivity, and greater efficiency than ever before. Interfaces such as DDR, PCIe, Ethernet, and other high-speed SerDes technologies continue to increase data rates while simultaneously reducing available design margins. At the same time, development schedules continue to shrink.</p>
<p>Engineering teams are no longer designing isolated circuits. They are designing highly interconnected systems where signal integrity, power integrity, thermal behavior, electromagnetic effects, and manufacturing constraints interact in ways that are increasingly difficult to predict through experience alone.</p>
<p>Traditional workflows remain heavily dependent upon a cycle of simulation, analysis, design modification, and revalidation. While this methodology has served the industry well, its scalability is being challenged by the sheer number of variables involved in modern designs.</p>
<p>The result is an industry-wide shift toward more intelligent systems that help engineers manage complexity without sacrificing accuracy or innovation.</p>
<h2>dBRICK as a representative design challenge</h2>
<p>The dBRICK design provides a useful example of the challenges facing modern electronic design teams. Originally developed as part of the European dRedBox initiative, dBRICK incorporates many characteristics common to contemporary high-performance systems. The design contains multiple PCIe Gen3 channels, high-density FPGA interfaces, complex differential routing structures, numerous vias and breakout regions, and multiple power delivery networks that must all operate together reliably.</p>
<p><img data-recalc-dims="1" loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-24280900" src="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig1-layout.webp?resize=1723%2C693&#038;ssl=1" alt="" width="1723" height="693" srcset="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig1-layout.webp?w=1723&amp;ssl=1 1723w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig1-layout.webp?resize=300%2C121&amp;ssl=1 300w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig1-layout.webp?resize=1024%2C412&amp;ssl=1 1024w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig1-layout.webp?resize=768%2C309&amp;ssl=1 768w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig1-layout.webp?resize=1536%2C618&amp;ssl=1 1536w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig1-layout.webp?resize=600%2C241&amp;ssl=1 600w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1000px) 100vw, 1000px" /></p>
<p><strong>Fig. 1: Layout view of dBRICK add-in card.</strong></p>
<p>While the specific application may differ from a networking appliance, industrial controller, aerospace platform, or data center accelerator, the engineering challenges are similar.</p>
<p>Designers must answer questions such as:</p>
<ul>
<li>How should differential pairs be routed to maintain signal integrity?</li>
<li>Which via structures provide the best electrical performance?</li>
<li>How should power delivery networks be optimized?</li>
<li>Which design variables have the greatest influence on performance?</li>
<li>How quickly can validation be completed within the development schedule?</li>
</ul>
<p>The answers are not always obvious because many design variables interact simultaneously. A modification that improves insertion loss may negatively impact impedance. A change that improves return loss may complicate manufacturing. Additional decoupling capacitors may improve power delivery while increasing cost and routing congestion.</p>
<p>This reality forms the foundation for understanding the roles of optimization, automation, and AI.</p>
<h2>Optimization: Managing an expanding design space</h2>
<p>Historically, engineers relied heavily on experience, design guidelines, and targeted simulations to identify acceptable solutions. As systems have grown more complex, this approach has become increasingly difficult to scale.</p>
<p>The challenge is no longer finding a design that works. The challenge is finding the best design among thousands of possibilities.</p>
<p>Consider a PCIe differential pair within the dBRICK design. Performance may be influenced by:</p>
<ul>
<li>Trace width</li>
<li>Trace separation</li>
<li>Dielectric thickness</li>
<li>Layer transitions</li>
<li>Via geometry</li>
<li>Pad dimensions</li>
<li>Anti-pad dimensions</li>
<li>Connector characteristics</li>
</ul>
<p>Each variable affects performance, and many of them interact with one another in non-linear ways. Changing trace width, for example, may improve insertion loss but also alter differential impedance. Adjusting via dimensions may improve one frequency range while degrading another. Small changes often produce effects that are difficult to predict through intuition alone.</p>
<p>This highlights one of the central challenges of modern engineering: human judgment remains valuable, but human intuition becomes less effective as the number of interacting variables increases.</p>
<p>Optimization addresses this challenge through systematic exploration of the design space. Instead of evaluating a handful of configurations, engineering teams can examine hundreds or thousands of possible combinations while measuring their effect on performance objectives. This allows designers to identify sensitivities, understand tradeoffs, and discover solution regions that might otherwise remain hidden. The same concept applies to power delivery networks.</p>
<p>Optimization techniques allow engineers to determine whether a design is truly under-engineered, appropriately engineered, or over-engineered. Optimization therefore represents much more than a simulation tool. It is a methodology for transforming engineering decisions from educated guesses into data-driven choices.</p>
<p><img data-recalc-dims="1" loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-24280901" src="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig2-risingedge.webp?resize=1570%2C674&#038;ssl=1" alt="" width="1570" height="674" srcset="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig2-risingedge.webp?w=1570&amp;ssl=1 1570w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig2-risingedge.webp?resize=300%2C129&amp;ssl=1 300w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig2-risingedge.webp?resize=1024%2C440&amp;ssl=1 1024w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig2-risingedge.webp?resize=768%2C330&amp;ssl=1 768w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig2-risingedge.webp?resize=1536%2C659&amp;ssl=1 1536w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig2-risingedge.webp?resize=600%2C258&amp;ssl=1 600w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1000px) 100vw, 1000px" /></p>
<p><strong>Fig. 2: Rising edge waveform results from swept parameter analysis.</strong></p>
<h2>Automation: Scaling engineering expertise</h2>
<p>While optimization improves decision quality, it does not solve another growing challenge: execution. Engineering organizations today perform more simulations than ever before.</p>
<p>Every design revision must be validated. Every critical interface must be analyzed. Every power rail must be evaluated. As products become more sophisticated, analysis workloads increase dramatically.</p>
<p>Consider a design containing dozens of high-speed channels. Simulations may need to be repeated across multiple design revisions, operating conditions, and manufacturing tolerances. Even when individual analyses are relatively straightforward, the total workload can become overwhelming. The bottleneck is often <strong>not</strong> computational power; the bottleneck is engineering time.</p>
<p>Another challenge involves the scalability of expertise. Many organizations possess highly experienced signal and power integrity specialists whose knowledge has been accumulated over decades. However, that expertise is often difficult to replicate across larger teams.</p>
<p>Two engineers performing the same task may:</p>
<ul>
<li>Use different assumptions</li>
<li>Select different software settings</li>
<li>Apply different validation criteria</li>
<li>Interpret requirements differently</li>
</ul>
<p>This introduces inconsistency into the engineering process.</p>
<p>As design complexity increases, so does the risk of human error. Incorrect model assignments, incomplete setup procedures, overlooked validation steps, and inconsistent reporting can all create costly delays. Automation addresses these challenges by embedding knowledge within repeatable workflows.</p>
<p>Rather than requiring engineers to manually configure every step of an analysis, automation ensures that critical procedures are executed consistently. Simulations can be launched in batches, results can be collected automatically, and validation processes can be standardized across projects and teams.</p>
<p>The value extends beyond productivity. Automation improves repeatability, reduces process variation, and enables engineering organizations to scale expertise more effectively. Best practices become part of the workflow rather than remaining dependent upon individual users.</p>
<p>Ultimately, automation allows engineers to spend less time managing processes and more time solving engineering problems.</p>
<h2>AI: Transforming data into insight</h2>
<p>If optimization helps engineers explore design possibilities and automation helps them execute workflows, AI addresses a different challenge entirely: understanding results.</p>
<p>Modern engineering tools generate enormous quantities of data. A single design may produce:</p>
<ul>
<li>Eye diagrams</li>
<li>Jitter measurements</li>
<li>S-parameters</li>
<li>Insertion loss curves</li>
<li>Return loss plots</li>
<li>Compliance reports</li>
<li>Power integrity analyses</li>
<li>Electromagnetic simulation results</li>
</ul>
<p>The problem facing many organizations is no longer data scarcity. It is information overload.</p>
<p>In the dBRICK example, engineers evaluating PCIe channels may generate dozens of reports for a single interface. Across an entire design, the volume of engineering data can quickly become overwhelming. Understanding what the results mean is considerably more difficult.</p>
<p>Many industries are experiencing shortages of highly specialized signal and power integrity experts, while simultaneously confronting increasingly sophisticated designs. As senior engineers retire and development complexity continues to grow, organizations face an expanding knowledge gap.</p>
<p>Perhaps the most difficult task in engineering is root-cause analysis. Determining that a design has failed is often relatively simple; determining why it failed is considerably harder.</p>
<p>A degraded eye pattern might originate from excessive channel loss, impedance discontinuities, crosstalk, poor power delivery, connector transitions, or some combination of these factors. Identifying the true source often requires extensive investigation.</p>
<p><img data-recalc-dims="1" loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-24280902" src="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig3-HTML.webp?resize=1409%2C440&#038;ssl=1" alt="" width="1409" height="440" srcset="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig3-HTML.webp?w=1409&amp;ssl=1 1409w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig3-HTML.webp?resize=300%2C94&amp;ssl=1 300w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig3-HTML.webp?resize=1024%2C320&amp;ssl=1 1024w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig3-HTML.webp?resize=768%2C240&amp;ssl=1 768w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig3-HTML.webp?resize=600%2C187&amp;ssl=1 600w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1000px) 100vw, 1000px" /></p>
<p><strong>Fig. 3: HTML results generated from SerDes Compliance Wizard showing eye height and width metrics.</strong></p>
<p>This is where AI has the potential to create substantial value. AI systems can help engineers navigate complexity by transforming large volumes of technical data into actionable insights.</p>
<p>In practical terms, AI may serve several roles:</p>
<ul>
<li>As a teacher, it can help users understand workflows and best practices.</li>
<li>As an assistant, it can guide engineers through complex setup procedures.</li>
<li>As an analyst, it can review simulation results and highlight potential concerns.</li>
<li>As an advisor, it can help identify likely root causes and suggest areas for further investigation.</li>
</ul>
<p>Within the dBRICK example, an AI-enabled workflow could examine PCIe compliance results and identify which portions of the channel contribute most significantly to signal degradation. Rather than spending hours investigating dozens of potential causes, engineers could focus immediately on the most likely contributors.</p>
<h2>Putting intelligent engineering into practice</h2>
<p>Many of these concepts are already being implemented within commercial engineering environments.</p>
<p>Siemens EDA&#8217;s HyperLynx platform provides examples of optimization capabilities through swept parameter analysis, power delivery network optimization, and multidimensional design space exploration. These technologies help engineers evaluate complex relationships between design variables and identify high-performing solutions.</p>
<p>The platform also incorporates numerous automation capabilities. Guided compliance workflows, protocol-aware analysis environments, scripting interfaces, and scalable execution frameworks reduce manual effort while improving consistency and repeatability across projects.</p>
<p>Emerging AI-driven functionality represents the next stage of this progression. Natural-language assistants can help users navigate workflows and access product knowledge more efficiently. AI-based analysis technologies are also being developed to help engineers interpret simulation results, identify potential issues, and accelerate root-cause investigation.</p>
<p><img data-recalc-dims="1" loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-24280903" src="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig4-Copilot.webp?resize=1721%2C801&#038;ssl=1" alt="" width="1721" height="801" srcset="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig4-Copilot.webp?w=1721&amp;ssl=1 1721w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig4-Copilot.webp?resize=300%2C140&amp;ssl=1 300w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig4-Copilot.webp?resize=1024%2C477&amp;ssl=1 1024w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig4-Copilot.webp?resize=768%2C357&amp;ssl=1 768w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig4-Copilot.webp?resize=1536%2C715&amp;ssl=1 1536w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Siemens_Int-Engineer-Optimiz-to-AI-fig4-Copilot.webp?resize=600%2C279&amp;ssl=1 600w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1000px) 100vw, 1000px" /></p>
<p><strong>Fig. 4: Copilot used in HyperLynx DRC to run rules on dBRICK.</strong></p>
<p>Taken together, these capabilities illustrate how the industry is moving beyond isolated simulations toward connected workflows that combine optimization, automation, and AI into a unified engineering process.</p>
<h2>Conclusion</h2>
<p>The growing complexity of modern electronic systems is forcing organizations to rethink traditional engineering methodologies. As design spaces expand, analysis workloads increase, and technical data volumes continue to grow, optimization, automation, and AI are becoming essential capabilities rather than optional enhancements.</p>
<p>Optimization enables engineers to make better decisions. Automation enables those decisions to be executed efficiently and consistently. AI enables engineers to transform data into actionable insight.</p>
<p>These technologies are not competing approaches. They are complementary layers of an emerging intelligent engineering framework.</p>
<p>Organizations that successfully integrate these capabilities into their development processes will be better equipped to manage complexity, accelerate innovation, improve product quality, and meet the demands of next-generation electronic systems.</p>
<p>The evolution toward intelligent engineering is already underway. The question facing most organizations is no longer whether optimization, automation, and AI will become part of engineering workflows, but how quickly they can be leveraged to create meaningful competitive advantage.</p>
<p>For a fuller analysis of how HyperLynx can support your team with optimization, automation, and AI, please read the new paper from Siemens, <a href="https://resources.sw.siemens.com/en-US/white-paper-the-evolution-of-intelligent-systems-from-optimization-to-automation-to-ai/">The evolution of intelligent systems: From optimization to automation to AI</a>.</p>

										<content:encoded><![CDATA[<p><strong>By Vincent van der Leest and Ajay Kapoor</strong></p>
<p>Security architects devote considerable attention to trusted processing, secure boot, and protected communications. Yet sensitive instructions and data often leave the system-on-chip (SoC) whenever they are written to external (off-chip) memory. That interface can expose mission-critical data like cryptographic material, sensor feeds, executable code, and intermediate processing results to physical probing or modification. For aerospace and government platforms, where systems may operate in contested environments and remain deployed for decades, the memory interface must be treated as part of the attack surface.</p>
<p>Inline Memory Encryption (IME) addresses this threat by encrypting data just before it is written to attached off-chip memory and decrypting when it returns for use. This is normally done by placing cryptographic processing directly in the path between processor logic and the memory controller. Because the operation is inline, protection can be transparent to software while keeping pace with the throughput and latency requirements of modern memory systems.</p>
<h2>Confidentiality is only half of the requirement</h2>
<p>Encryption prevents an attacker from understanding the data observed on a memory bus or recovered from an external DRAM device. It does not necessarily prove that the data has not been altered. This distinction is critical. A hostile actor may not need to read mission data if blindly changing data associated with a command, coordinate, lookup table, or executable instruction is enough to influence system behavior.</p>
<p>The same principle applies beyond aerospace and government systems: manipulated medical or financial data can affect critical decisions, while altered training data, model parameters, or inference inputs can poison an AI system. Confidentiality prevents disclosure and complicates specific edits; authentication determines whether the data can still be trusted.</p>
<p>Authentication should therefore occur as close as possible to where data is stored and before that data is consumed. This minimizes the path over which unauthenticated information can travel and helps prevent corrupted data from propagating into processors, accelerators, or downstream control logic. IME should be viewed not only as a confidentiality feature, but as an important contributor to overall system integrity and robustness.</p>
<h2>Different AES operation modes for IME</h2>
<p>AES-XTS is widely used for high-throughput storage and memory encryption. Its location-dependent tweak ensures that identical plaintext blocks stored at different addresses produce different ciphertext without increasing the protected data size. However, AES-XTS can protect the data confidentiality only and is not a type of Authenticated Encryption with Associated Data (AEAD), i.e. one that ensures both the confidentiality and authentication of memory contents.</p>
<p>AES-GCM combines confidentiality with integrity verification, allowing an authentication tag to be checked before plaintext is released. This provides protection against deliberate tampering. However, it requires storage and management of authentication tags and nonces or initialization vectors, which can affect memory capacity, bandwidth, address mapping, caching, and initialization.</p>
<p>The decision is therefore not simply a comparison of cipher strength. Architects must align the mode with the threat model, assurance objective, acceptable memory overhead, performance target, and wider system architecture. For many mission-critical systems, the ability to cryptographically detect modification justifies the additional metadata and design complexity of authenticated encryption.</p>
<h2>Cryptographic strength requires implementation resilience</h2>
<p>Secure algorithms still require resilient implementations. Power, electromagnetic, timing, and other physical leakage can enable side-channel analysis to recover secret keys without breaking AES mathematically, especially when adversaries have physical access to equipment.</p>
<p>Side-channel countermeasures should be designed into the cipher implementation rather than treated as a later system-level add-on. Secure key handling, predictable error behavior, protected control paths, and robustness against random faults also contribute to the effective assurance of the complete engine.</p>
<p>For aerospace and government programs, a field-proven solution and a supplier with practical anti-tamper experience can reduce both implementation risk and the burden of establishing confidence in the design.</p>
<h2>Meeting requirements for secure off-chip memory</h2>
<p>The Rambus <a href="https://www.rambus.com/security/inline-memory-encryption/ime-ip-340/">IME-IP-340</a> addresses these requirements with authenticated memory protection, high performance, secure key handling, and field-proven side-channel resistance. Designed for FPGA deployments, it sits between processor logic and the memory controller, using AES-GCM to encrypt, decrypt, and authenticate memory transactions with high throughput and low latency.</p>
<p><img data-recalc-dims="1" loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-24280946" src="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Rambus_Protecting-Mission-Critical-Data-Beyond-SoC-Fig1.webp?resize=1385%2C775&#038;ssl=1" alt="" width="1385" height="775" srcset="https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Rambus_Protecting-Mission-Critical-Data-Beyond-SoC-Fig1.webp?w=1385&amp;ssl=1 1385w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Rambus_Protecting-Mission-Critical-Data-Beyond-SoC-Fig1.webp?resize=300%2C168&amp;ssl=1 300w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Rambus_Protecting-Mission-Critical-Data-Beyond-SoC-Fig1.webp?resize=1024%2C573&amp;ssl=1 1024w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Rambus_Protecting-Mission-Critical-Data-Beyond-SoC-Fig1.webp?resize=768%2C430&amp;ssl=1 768w, https://i0.wp.com/semiengineering.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Rambus_Protecting-Mission-Critical-Data-Beyond-SoC-Fig1.webp?resize=600%2C336&amp;ssl=1 600w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1000px) 100vw, 1000px" /></p>
<p><strong>Fig. 1: Block diagram of Rambus IME-IP-340.</strong></p>
<p>The engine includes configurable memory management, cache support, native key management, and optional power-analysis side-channel attack (“SCA”) countermeasures. These capabilities help designers balance security, performance, memory overhead, and integration requirements while maintaining a dedicated security boundary in the memory path.</p>
<h2>Extending protection across the system lifecycle</h2>
<p>There are additional system-level challenges that must be looked at together between system- and IME design teams for a successful IME solution:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Data freshness:</strong> Preventing replay of older, correctly authenticated values may require protected counters, version information, or synchronized state, with implications for metadata, persistence, and recovery.</li>
<li><strong>Memory initialization:</strong> Protected regions may need valid metadata before normal operation, and initializing large memories can affect boot time and system availability.</li>
<li><strong>Root of Trust integration:</strong> Secure key provisioning, security policy, lifecycle transitions, zeroization, and recovery should form part of a coherent chain of trust from secure boot through runtime memory protection.</li>
</ul>
<p>Beyond providing the cryptographic engine, Rambus helps customers address freshness, initialization, key provisioning, and Root of Trust integration. This system-level support helps balance assurance, performance, boot time, memory, and lifecycle requirements across the wider platform security architecture.</p>
<h2>Building trust beyond the chip boundary</h2>
<p>Protecting off-chip memory in mission-critical systems requires more than hiding its contents. Data must remain confidential, unauthorized modifications must be detected before corrupted information is used, and the implementation itself must remain resilient against relevant physical attacks.</p>
<p>More broadly, memory protection illustrates an important architectural principle: security cannot stop at the boundary of a trusted processing component. When sensitive information leaves that boundary, the system must determine what protections are required along the path and before the information is consumed. Confidentiality, integrity, authentication, key management, and lifecycle security therefore need to be considered as elements of the broader system security architecture.</p>
<p>Authenticated, side-channel-resistant IME applies that principle to a particularly important boundary: the path between processing and external memory and is an important building block for resilient aerospace and government architectures.</p>
<h3>Links</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://www.rambus.com/security/inline-memory-encryption/">Rambus Inline Memory Encryption IP</a></li>
<li>White paper: <a href="https://go.rambus.com/hardware-deployment-for-secure-ai-using-confidential-computing">Hardware Deployment for Secure AI Using Confidential Computing</a></li>
</ul>
<p><em>Ajay Kapoor is senior principal engineer systems architect for Rambus Security IP. He is lead architect and product manager for the Inline Memory Encryption IP product portfolio. He holds more than 25 patents, and his work has been included in more than 15 scientific publications. Kapoor holds a MSc from Twente University and a BTech Electrical Engineering from the Indian Institute of Technology (IIT).</em></p>

]]></content:encoded>

		<item>
		<title>Building Trust Into Physical AI Systems</title>
		<link>https://semiengineering.com/building-trust-into-physical-ai-systems/</link>
					<comments>https://semiengineering.com/building-trust-into-physical-ai-systems/#respond</comments>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Dana Neustadter]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 03 Sep 2026 07:03:52 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Blogs - Security]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[IoT and Security]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Safe, Secure, Smarter Cars]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[physical AI]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[robotics]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[sensors]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Synopsys]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">https://semiengineering.com/?p=24280964</guid>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Security and safety are inseparable in the physical world.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://semiengineering.com/building-trust-into-physical-ai-systems/">Building Trust Into Physical AI Systems</a> appeared first on <a href="https://semiengineering.com">Semiconductor Engineering</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[<p><strong>By Dana Neustadter and Ilya Tolchinsky</strong></p>
<p>Artificial intelligence is moving beyond digital experiences and into systems that interact directly with the physical world.</p>
<p>This emerging category of physical AI spans autonomous vehicles that evaluate road conditions in real time, industrial robots that adjust to changing factory environments, drones that navigate complex airspace, surgical systems that support clinicians, and intelligent infrastructure that senses and responds without human intervention.</p>
<p>Unlike conventional AI applications, these systems must do more than process information or generate content. They must accurately perceive their surroundings, make decisions, and translate those decisions into real-world actions. As a result, precision and reliability are fundamental requirements.</p>
<p>An incorrect output from a digital AI model may lead to an inaccurate answer, a poor recommendation, or a disappointing customer experience. In physical AI, an error can cause a system to overlook an obstacle, mishandle machinery, brake unexpectedly, or introduce serious operational and human risks.</p>
<p>Securing physical AI therefore requires more than protecting the network or validating model accuracy. Trust must extend across the complete path from perception and decision-making to physical action.</p>
<p>Understanding the physical AI threat landscape</p>
<p>The security risks in physical AI begin well before inference and extend well beyond software.</p>
<p>One important vulnerability lies in training data. If a system is trained on incomplete, biased, manipulated, or low-quality data — whether real or synthetic — it can learn the wrong patterns from the start. That may not be obvious in testing, but it can surface later in operation, when a robot misjudges spacing on a factory floor, a drone fails to interpret an unfamiliar environment, or an autonomous system responds incorrectly to a real-world condition it should have recognized.</p>
<p>Another challenge is that physical AI depends on sensors that directly connect software to the world. Cameras, lidar, radar, microphones, and positioning systems all shape how the model perceives its environment. If those inputs are degraded, spoofed, blocked, or manipulated, the system’s understanding of its environment can shift with them.</p>
<p>That risk grows when multiple functions must operate together in real time. A robotic platform may rely on one component to perceive its surroundings, another to plan movement, and another to execute control. If those functions lose synchronization because of latency, inconsistent inputs, model drift, or a compromised subsystem, the result can be unpredictable behavior.</p>
<p>Hardware adds another layer of exposure. Physical AI systems rely on silicon, embedded IP, accelerators, and interconnects that form the foundation for everything above them. If that foundation is weak, higher-level protections may not hold. A flaw in a hardware root of trust, an insecure firmware update path, or vulnerable IP reused across products can expose the entire system.</p>
<p>The rise of agentic AI makes these challenges even more complex. As physical AI systems navigate dynamic environments, make decisions, and adapt their behavior in real time, securing that behavior becomes more difficult and the attack surface expands.</p>
<p>That dynamic behavior requires continuous runtime assurance. AI models cannot be validated once before deployment and assumed to remain trustworthy indefinitely. In physical AI systems, model behavior must be checked continuously to ensure outputs remain within safe operating bounds as conditions change. Without that ongoing validation, small shifts in inputs, environments, or system behavior can cascade into larger operational and safety risks.</p>
<p><strong>Adopting system-level design and assurance</strong><br />
The lesson is straightforward: for physical AI, trust cannot be confined to the application layer. It must be designed into every layer, from silicon to software to the full system.</p>
<p>A strong model is not enough if the hardware underneath is vulnerable. Hardened hardware is not enough if the data pipeline is compromised. Reliable perception is not enough if runtime safeguards fail in unfamiliar conditions.</p>
<p>The next era of AI will require system-level design and assurance, with continuous monitoring.</p>
<p>That means examining how confidence is established across the full lifecycle of a system: where data comes from, how model integrity is validated, how hardware and software supply chains are secured, how updates are authenticated, and how the system behaves when conditions become uncertain or conflicting.</p>
<p>Physical AI will not be defined by capability alone. It will be defined by assurance and proof of provenance. Systems must be able to perceive accurately, act safely, resist manipulation, and remain within trusted boundaries as conditions change.</p>
<p>The organizations that lead will be the ones that treat security and safety as inseparable and build trust from the ground up.</p>
<p><strong>— Ilya Tolchinsky is a principal product manager for AI at Synopsys.</strong></p>
<p>The post <a href="https://semiengineering.com/building-trust-into-physical-ai-systems/">Building Trust Into Physical AI Systems</a> appeared first on <a href="https://semiengineering.com">Semiconductor Engineering</a>.</p>
]]></content:encoded>
